| 197 | **CML's report-version lookup throws a `ClassCastException` on every CML view, so a CML lab never shows its other versions.** `MDSResultsData.getMatchingCMLLabs` (`:586-589`) casts the second and third columns of `LabPatientPhysicianInfoDaoImpl.findLabServiceDatesByLabId` (`:85-95`) to `Date`, but both are `lpp.serviceDate`, a `String` property (`LabPatientPhysicianInfo:64`, column `service_date varchar(10)`). The exception is caught, logged at ERROR (`:610`) and the method returns only the requested lab, so `CMLLabTest.populateLab` always gets a one-lab chain: the `Version:` links never appear, an acknowledgement never files the older versions of the same accession, and a forward, which also resolves its chain through `getMatchingLabs` (`CommonLabResultData:809`), would reach one version only (source; not driven live). `CommonLabResultData.getMatchingLabs` calls it on every CML acknowledge (`olderVersionsOf`, `:552`), where the journal logged one error per acknowledgement; the strict form `getMatchingLabsForMutation` (`:962`, called from `MrpRoutingService:180`) calls `getMatchingCMLLabs(labNo, true)`, which would rethrow the same cast as an `IllegalStateException` (`MDSResultsData:609`) (source; not driven live) | Live: every open of an owned CML lab wrote `ERROR data.MDSResultsData (MDSResultsData.java:610) - exception in getMatchingCMLLabs (ClassCastException)` to the journal (one per open, and one per acknowledgement), while the page itself rendered normally. A second CML version was not seeded, so the missing version links are from reading the source. Not asserted by the `legacy-lab-display` checks. Filed as #4568. **Fixed (2026-10-15, #4568):** `getMatchingCMLLabs` parses the `service_date` strings (the vendor's `YYYYMMDD`, strictly, so `20260230` or trailing text is refused) and applies the four-month window the MDS, PathNet and HL7 chains use; reports tied on print date and time are ordered by id (import order). A report whose date cannot be read is left out of the chain, never assumed to belong, because acknowledging or forwarding files or routes every version in it; the strict form that patient matching uses refuses (`IllegalStateException`) instead, so a mutation never acts on some versions of an order and not the others. The strict form always threw for CML: on the packaged install with the pre-fix classes, choosing a patient in the matching popup for a CML report answered HTTP 500 from `oscarMDS/PatientMatch`. A report with a blank accession number has no other versions (the lookup excludes it from matching: a blank accession equals every other blank one, which would have chained unrelated patients' reports now that the chain works), and the selected report is always its own chain, alone when the lookup cannot place it (no print header). Pinned by `MDSResultsDataCmlVersionChainUnitTest`, `MDSResultsDataCmlVersionChainIntegrationTest`, `LabPatientPhysicianInfoDaoImplUnitTest`, the `LabPatientPhysicianInfoDao` version-chain tests and the `cml-version-chain` browser check, which fails at its Display step on the pre-fix classes and at its Match step when only the Match step is run. | fixed |

### Found by the write-side role matrix and the CSRF negative matrix

From `authz-write-role-matrix` and `csrf-negative-matrix` (Task 11 of the 2026.08 coverage plan),
run on the packaged Ontario install (`carlos-emr 2026.08.0~alpha19`, demo data) at fefe5504ea.
Each mutation was captured from the full-privilege login's own UI action and replayed through
`lib/mutation-replay.js`: with the cookies and the valid CSRF token of a login holding only
`_demographic` r, `_appointment` w and `_eChart` r (and the test login's programs), or with the
same session and no token. The CSRF matrix found no defect: all eleven families and the `/ws/rs`
content-type probes were refused.

| # | Defect | Evidence | Status |
|---|---|---|---|
| 198 | **A role with no note rights can write a chart note; the "refusal" comes after the write.** `CaseManagementEntry2Action.execute` checks only `_demographic` r (`:154`) before dispatching `save` (`:172`, method `:1850`), `ajaxsave` (`:174`, method `:1884`) and `saveAndExit` (`:178`, method `:2144`), and `noteSave` (`:1477`) checks only the session's note lock (`hasNoteLock`, `:2086`); no `_casemgmt.notes` or `_eChart` write check exists in `casemgmt/web` or `CaseManagementManagerImpl`. The only `_casemgmt.notes` gate on this path is in the view the `list` result chains to (`struts-clinical.xml:175`, `/CaseManagementView?method=view`, then `ChartNotes.jsp:106-109`), which redirects to `securityError` after `noteSave()` has committed. `saveAndExit` (Sign & Save) ends in `close.jsp` (`:2245`) or a redirect to the bill form (`:2175-2230`) and reaches no such view (source; not driven live) | Live, `authz-write-role-matrix` step "chart note save: the restricted login's replay with its own valid token is refused, and the full login's replay writes": the restricted login opened the patient's chart (a read it holds), replayed the full login's captured Save with its own valid token, and got `HTTP 302` to `/securityError?type=_casemgmt.notes` while `casemgmt_note` gained the replayed note (COUNT 0 to 1); the same request from the full login also wrote. In a one-off probe with the same fixture, a role holding `_casemgmt.notes` r (read only) was shown the note editor, Save and the Bill button, and its own Save wrote the note (HTTP 200). Without program membership the same saves fail with HTTP 500 (`UnexpectedRollbackException` / `OptimisticLockException` incidents) before any authorization decision. Authorization defect for a signed-in user: tracked in umbrella [#4223](https://github.com/carlos-emr/carlos/issues/4223), section A. | issue-filed |
| 199 | **The billing, eForm and messenger Struts packages turn an authorization refusal into an HTTP 500 "unexpected error".** Thirteen of the seventeen module files map `java.lang.SecurityException` to `securityError` (for example `struts-clinical.xml:37`), which CarlosExceptionMappingInterceptor answers with a 403; `struts-billing.xml` (packages `billing`, `:29`, and `billing-on`, `:373-416`), `struts-eform.xml` and `struts-messenger.xml` map none (nor does `struts-login.xml`), so a `SecurityException` thrown by their actions (for example `BillingOnSave2Action:91-92`, `missing required sec object (_billing)`) falls through to the container's error page with status 500. The refusal itself works (nothing is written), but the browser and the log see a server fault: `DefaultDispatcherErrorHandler` logs it at ERROR with the trace, and a probe cannot tell it from a crash | Live, `authz-write-role-matrix-billing` (the same script with `AUTHZ_WRITE_ONLY=billing-on-save`, which pins this finding), step "Ontario bill save: the restricted login's replay with its own valid token is refused, and the full login's replay writes": the write-restricted login's replay of a captured Ontario bill save (`billing/CA/ON/BillingONSave`, valid token) answered HTTP 500 with the CARLOS error page and no `billing_on_cheader1` row, and the journal logged `ERROR dispatcher.DefaultDispatcherErrorHandler ... missing required sec object (_billing)` and `errorpage.jsp captured exception (... status=500)`; the same request from the full login wrote its bill. `authz-read-role-matrix` fails its last step on the same packages (its manifest notes: 70 read routes answer a login with no sec object with 500). In the full `authz-write-role-matrix` run the bill step comes after the note-save step, so it is reached only once finding 198 is fixed. Authorization refusal handling for a signed-in user: tracked in umbrella [#4223](https://github.com/carlos-emr/carlos/issues/4223), section B. | issue-filed |
| 200 | **The chart's Sign, Save & Bill button is shown to a role without billing rights.** `ChartNotes.jsp:634-641` renders the dollar-sign Bill input whenever `bean.source == null`, with no `_billing` check (the schedule's Bill link is gated: `appointmentprovideradminday.jsp:454-456` sets `billingRights` from `_billing` r). Pressing it signs and saves the note and sends the user to the bill form, which the role cannot use (finding 199) | Live, `authz-write-chart-bill` (the `authz-write-role-matrix` script with `AUTHZ_WRITE_MODE=chart-bill`, which pins this finding), step "the restricted chart's note toolbar offers no Bill button to a role without _billing" (the step before it holds the controls): a login holding `_demographic` r, `_appointment` w, `_eChart` r and `_casemgmt.notes` r (no `_billing`) is shown the note toolbar (note box and Save) and the Bill button in it; the full login's chart shows it too (control). `authz-write-role-matrix` itself cannot judge Bill: its role has no note rights, so `ChartNotes.jsp:106-109` redirects the notes panel to `securityError` and no note toolbar renders. Authorization refusal handling for a signed-in user: tracked in umbrella [#4223](https://github.com/carlos-emr/carlos/issues/4223), section B. | issue-filed |
| 201 | **A role that may open the chart but lacks the CPP rights gets four "Error: 500" boxes in it.** `CaseManagementView2Action.listNotes` (`:1034`) returns `SUCCESS` when the role lacks `_newCasemgmt.otherMeds`, `.riskFactors`, `.familyHistory` or `.medicalHistory` (`:1056-1076`; the comments say "The link ... won't show up"), but the `CaseManagementView` action maps no `success` result (`struts-clinical.xml:159-171`), so Struts throws a `ConfigurationException`, CarlosExceptionMappingInterceptor logs it at ERROR (`:197`, no frame or path in the server-log audit's signature) and the box renders the 500 body. The read refusal just above returns `null` (`:1049-1050`), which renders nothing | Live: the `authz-write-role-matrix` restricted login (`_demographic` r, `_appointment` w, `_eChart` r) opening a patient's chart: its navbar load state records the FamHistory, OMeds and RiskFactors modules as failed, the chart shows "Error: 500" in those boxes and the Medical History box, and the journal logs four `Unhandled org.apache.struts2.config.ConfigurationException ... in action CaseManagementView (POST /carlos/CaseManagementView)` per opening, which `scripts/deb-server-log-audit.sh` reports as UNKNOWN. None was logged on the install before this check ran (12:00-16:50 that day) Authorization refusal handling for a signed-in user: tracked in umbrella [#4223](https://github.com/carlos-emr/carlos/issues/4223), section B. | issue-filed |
| 202 | **`OscarDownload` serves any file of the document store to a login that opened the OBEC report page, so a login holding only `_report` r, and no `_edoc`, can read stored documents through it.** `OscarDownload.doGet` checks no privilege: it serves the directory a session attribute names (`OscarDownload.java:79-90`; the only other condition is `user != null`, `:82`). `obec.jsp:84-85` sets `obecdownload` to `DOCUMENT_DIR` for any session that opens the OBEC page (`Obec2Action.java:73` needs `_report` r), and `DOCUMENT_DIR` is where the OBEC output is written (`ObecData.java:108-112`, `OBECE<millis>.TXT`) and also where every stored patient document lives. The servlet does not limit the name to OBEC output, so any file in the store can be downloaded by name, bypassing the document manager's `_edoc` check (`ManageDocument2Action.java:1188-1190`) and its per-patient check. The seeded `admin` role (`_report` x, no `_edoc`: `V1.0.2__on_data.sql:35920`, and only `doctor` has `_edoc`, `:36085`) is affected too, but it also holds `_admin` x and can grant itself `_edoc`, so the pin uses a role holding `_report` r alone. A file name has to be known or guessed; whether a role without document access can learn names was not tested. | Live, `direct-response-contract` step "OscarDownload (OBEC key) refuses a stored-document file to a login that holds no _edoc": a throwaway login whose custom role holds exactly `_report` r (nothing it could use to grant itself rights) opened `oscarReport/obec` (HTTP 200) and requested `servlet/OscarDownload?homepath=obecdownload&filename=<marker>-doc.pdf`, a FAKE marker file the check had placed in `DOCUMENT_DIR`: HTTP 200 with `Content-Disposition: attachment` and the 141 exact bytes. In the step before it, the same login is served an OBEC-shaped marker (`OBECE` + 13 digits + `.TXT`) through the same key, so the session is armed and the directory readable, and `documentManager/ManageDocument?method=display` refuses it with the application's 403. If the fix limits the key to the files the application generates, those controls stay valid; a fix of another kind (a per-file token, a privilege check) changes them. Authorization defect for a signed-in user: tracked in umbrella [#4223](https://github.com/carlos-emr/carlos/issues/4223), section A. | issue-filed |
| 203 | **`BackupDownload` and `OscarDownload` answer a request for a file that is not in the directory with a bare HTTP 500 and an uncaught exception.** After the name passes validation, `GenericDownload.transferFile` sets the download headers (`:148-151`) and opens the file with `new FileInputStream` (`:154`); the resulting `FileNotFoundException` is an `IOException`, which both servlets rethrow (`BackupDownload.java:90-91`, `OscarDownload.java:97-98`) instead of answering through the `sendError` paths they use for every other refusal. The container then writes its own "Error 500" page (309 bytes, not the application's error page) and `ResponseSanitizationFilter` logs `Uncaught exception escaped filter chain` with the stack at ERROR. The 500 does not carry the `Content-Disposition: attachment` set before the open: the container resets the response, and the probe saw `text/html` and no Content-Disposition. A missing file (a backup deleted between listing and click, a mistyped name) should be a 404 or 400 the way a bad name is; by analogy with CLAUDE.md's "Direct Response Actions" rule (which is written for Struts direct-response actions, and these are servlets), a direct-response route owns its error response. On the packaged install the same 500 answers every `BackupDownload` request that uses `backup_path` or the built-in default directory, because the shipped `backup_path=/home/mysql/` is hidden from the service by `ProtectHome=yes` (`FileNotFoundException ... (Permission denied)`; the Backup Download page itself says "Backup directory not found"). | Live, one-off probes while building `direct-response-contract` (the check does not repeat them, so a run does not add an ERROR stack to the journal): an administrator whose session was armed by `billing/CA/ON/moveMOHFiles?folder=INBOX` requested `servlet/BackupDownload?filename=<marker>-missing.txt`, and one armed by `oscarReport/obec` requested `servlet/OscarDownload?homepath=obecdownload&filename=<marker>-missing.txt`: both HTTP 500, `text/html`, 309 bytes, no Content-Disposition, carrying the application header; the journal logged `ERROR utility.ResponseSanitizationFilter ... Uncaught exception escaped filter chain [uri=/carlos/servlet/BackupDownload ...]` and `/servlet/OscarDownload` with `ServletException: java.io.FileNotFoundException: ... (No such file or directory)`. Filed as #4569. | issue-filed |
| 204 | **Every request for an unmapped action is written to the journal in full, query string included, at WARN.** Struts' `Dispatcher` (struts2-core 7.1.1, `Dispatcher.java:787`) answers a request that matches no action with a 404 and, with devMode off, logs `Could not find action or result: <request URI>?<query string>` at WARN with a stack trace (the class builds the text from `getRequestURI()` and `getQueryString()`). The query string is whatever the caller sent: a stale bookmark, a removed route or a mistyped link opened from a chart carries `demographic_no`, and the legacy pages also put names and health numbers there. The page the user sees is clean ("CARLOS Error: 404"); the journal is not, and neither the ERROR-only `scripts/deb-server-log-audit.sh` nor a response-body check reads a WARN. CLAUDE.md asks that PHI is never logged and that operational identifiers pass through `LogSafe`; this line is written by framework code, so a fix belongs in the logging configuration (the `org.apache.struts2.dispatcher.Dispatcher` logger) or a filter in front of the dispatcher, not in an action | Live, `phi-in-error-pages` step "the server log holds none of this run HIN, FAKE- names, address or e-mail": ten GETs to unmapped `NoSuch...Action` routes, one under each of the ten route families (demographic, chart, Rx, lab, document, tickler, billing, eForm, messenger, administration), each carrying the owned patient's `demographic_no`, a synthetic HIN and a FAKE- last name in the query, were answered with the clean 404 page; `scripts/deb-server-log-phi-scan.sh --only-given` over the run's window then reported `HIT journal WARN dispatcher.Dispatcher hin events=10` and `HIT journal WARN dispatcher.Dispatcher marker events=10` (counts and logger only). The events were read once with the values masked: `Could not find action or result: /carlos/<route>?demographic_no=<n>&hin=<HIN>&last_name=<name>`. The same query sent to mapped routes (the 400, 403 and 405 probes) and to the 404s that mapped routes answer themselves (`eform/displayImage`, the REST resources) logged none of the three. The constants `Could not find action or result`, `getQueryString` and `isDevMode` are in the packaged struts2-core-7.1.1.jar. Filed as #4570. Fixed on `release/2026.08`: `log4j2.xml` puts a `RegexFilter` (DENY on `Could not find action or result.*`) on the `org.apache.struts2.dispatcher.Dispatcher` logger, so that one framework message is dropped at every `LOG_VERBOSITY` while other Dispatcher output stays; the 404 remains in the Tomcat access log (`%U`, no query string). `Log4j2DispatcherNotFoundFilterUnitTest` loads the shipped config; the `phi-in-error-pages` `expectedFailure` is removed. | `fixed` |
| 205 | **A page gated by several security objects admits or refuses a role depending on the order the database happens to return its rows, so the seeded `doctor` loses the OBEC report page when two unrelated rows exist.** `<security:oscarSec objectName="_report,_admin.reporting,_billing">` (`obec.jsp:40`; `SecurityTag.java:69`) passes the whole list to `OscarRoleObjectPrivilege.getPrivilegeProp`, which keeps ONE privilege per role: `prop.setProperty(roleUserGroup, privilege)` (`:67`) overwrites it with each row, so the LAST row returned for the role wins across all the listed objects. The rows come from `SecObjPrivilegeDaoImpl.findByObjectNames` (`:65`), `order by s.priority desc`, and every priority is 0, so the order of ties is whatever the engine's filesort produces. The `doctor` role holds `_report` x and `_billing` x and an explicit `_admin.reporting` o (no rights; `V1.0.6__restore_reporting_privilege.sql:3`), so the page admits the doctor when the `o` row comes first and refuses it (`checkRights` finds the bare `o` outside the r/u/w hierarchy and falls back to the exact-match comparison, `privilegeLower.equals(rightsLower)`, which is false for a required `r`, `OscarRoleObjectPrivilege.java:213`) when it comes last. Every page with a multi-object list is affected in the same way, for any role holding different privileges on the listed objects; the single-object `hasPrivilege` checks in the actions are not. | Live: with the install's rows alone the doctor opens `oscarReport/obec` (HTTP 200, `direct-response-contract` first runs). With two unrelated rows added (a role holding `_report` r and another holding `_admin.reporting` r, which is what the check's least-privilege logins create) the same doctor's request answered HTTP 302 to `/carlos/securityError` while the action's own single-object check (`Obec2Action.java:73`) had passed. The row order shows why: `SELECT roleUserGroup, objectName, privilege FROM secObjPrivilege WHERE objectName IN ('_report','_admin.reporting','_billing') ORDER BY priority DESC` returns the doctor's rows as `_admin.reporting o`, `_billing x`, `_report x` with the install's rows alone or with either extra row, and as `_report x`, `_billing x`, `_admin.reporting o` with both extra rows (the extra rows were deleted again). `direct-response-contract` therefore creates its least-privilege roles only after the steps that use the doctor on that page. Not pinned by a check of its own. Authorization defect for a signed-in user: tracked in umbrella [#4223](https://github.com/carlos-emr/carlos/issues/4223), section A. | issue-filed |

| 209 | **A letter saved from the Rich Text Letter eForm is refused by the WAF when its first line is a pasted internal link, because exclusion 1045 does not unhook `attack-rfi` on `ARGS:Letter`.** The Rich Text Letter posts the whole letter as `Letter`: the editor's HTML, entity-escaped by the form's `saveRTL()`. A first line typed into the editor is not wrapped in a tag, so the posted value STARTS with whatever the clinician typed. CRS 931100 (`attack-rfi`, CRITICAL at paranoia level 1) is anchored on the start of the argument and fires on a value that begins with a URL whose host is an IP address, which is how a clinic pastes a link to its own PACS or intranet. Exclusion 1045 (`REQUEST-900-EXCLUSION-RULES-BEFORE-CRS.conf:294-303`) unhooks six tag families from `ARGS:Letter` and leaves `attack-rfi` out, taking over the reasoning of the eForm editor rule 1050 (`:383-392`), where the argument is an HTML document that cannot begin with a URL; a letter can. The other phrases of the corpus pass, because the other families are unhooked. The save answers nginx's bare 403, the typed letter is lost, and the application logs nothing. A fix is `ctl:ruleRemoveTargetByTag=attack-rfi;ARGS:Letter` on rule 1045 | Live, `waf-clinical-text-corpus-eform-letter` (2026-10-08, packaged 2026.08.0~alpha19 through `:443`, `EXPECT_FRONT_DOOR=true`): the corpus typed into the Rich Text Letter editor with the PACS-link phrase as the first line, sent with the toolbar Submit (`POST /eform/addEForm`), was answered 403. The ModSecurity audit log names 931100 'Possible Remote File Inclusion (RFI) Attack: URL Parameter using IP Address' on `ARGS:Letter` (matched text `http://10.0.0.5`, the start of the posted value) and the 949110 total, and no other signature. The control step, a plain one-line letter through the same route, saves, and so does the corpus with the PACS-link line left out: the other six phrases are typed, submitted and stored exactly, so the link line alone is what the WAF refuses. Static: `waf-clinical-text-corpus.test.js` `shouldUnhookAttackRfiOnLetter_whenTheRichTextLetterIsSaved` is a todo that starts passing when the conf unhooks it. Filed as #4571. **Fixed (2026-10-14, #4571):** Rule 1045 now exempts attack-rfi for Letter on the existing POST route. On 2026-10-14 the unchanged policy reproduced 931100 with the plain-text control passing; the updated policy passed the full corpus through the real editor and stored it exactly. The expected-failure pin is removed. | fixed |

The 403 is silent in the box (the stash keeps the previous name) and the same text goes out again at Save Only (finding 211). | 211 | **Save Only in Rx is refused by the WAF when the sig, the drug name or the comment of a card holds prose, because the exclusions cover the post made when the box loses focus and not the one made at Save.** Save Only posts every card's boxes in one request, `rx/WriteScript?parameterValue=updateSaveAllDrugs`, under names that carry the card's random id: `instructions_<n>` (the sig), `drugName_<n>` and `comment_<n>` (`RxWriteScript2Action.java:1501`, `:1529`, `:1630`). (HTTP 403)' alert. The control step (sig `1 tab PO BID x 14 days`, Save Only) lands in `drugs.special`, and the special-instruction row, whose Save does not carry the text, passes. Static: `waf-clinical-text-corpus.test.js` `shouldUnhookTheSaveOnlyBoxes_whenAPrescriptionHoldsProse` is a todo that starts passing only when `missingForSaveOnlyBoxes` is empty, that is when none of the 12 box and family combinations is still inspected: all three boxes (`drugName_<n>`, `instructions_<n>`, `comment_<n>`) unhooked on `rx/WriteScript` for all four families (attack-lfi, attack-rfi, attack-rce, attack-injection-php). | 212 | **A clinical `<?` that the application sends back in a JSON answer makes the WAF drop that answer: a Vitals comment saves but the browser gets no reply, and a tickler containing it stops the tickler list from loading.** CRS 953120 ('PHP source code leakage', phase 4, `RESPONSE-953-DATA-LEAKAGES-PHP.conf`) matches `<\?(?!xml)` anywhere in a response body. The packaged WAF inspects text/plain, text/html, text/xml and application/json responses (`main.conf:77-78`) and blocks at an outbound score of 4 (`crs-overrides.conf:38`), which one 953120 match reaches. The prose exclusions work on the request only. HTML pages encode `<` as `&lt;`, but JSON does not, so the two JSON answers that repeat stored prose carry it raw: the Vitals save (`POST /encounter/Measurements`, `{"encounterText":"BP ..."}`) and the DataTables tickler list (`GET /tickler/ListTicklers`, `data[].message`). By phase 4 nginx has sent the headers, so it cannot answer 403: its error log says `header already sent while sending to client` and the connection is dropped. The Vitals row IS written and the clinician sees a failed request and may submit again; a tickler saved with `<?` in its message (let in by exclusion 1104) stays in the list, so every load of a list that includes that tickler fails for as long as it is among the rows asked for (the Active list until it is completed, the Completed list afterwards) | Live, `waf-clinical-text-corpus-measurement-comment` (2026-10-08, packaged install through `:443`): the Vitals Submit with the wound-measurement phrase ('Wound <2cm, clean. <?> follow up in 1 week.') in the comment passes the request and the `measurements` row lands exactly, but no answer arrives (Playwright: socket hang up). Audit log: 953120 on `RESPONSE_BODY` (matched text `<?` in `{"encounterText":"BP ...`) and 959100 'Outbound Anomaly Score Exceeded (Total Score: 4)'. The same Submit with the other six phrases is answered normally. A probe the same day stored each phrase in turn as a tickler message (by SQL): `GET /tickler/ListTicklers` answered 200 with the row for six phrases and was dropped, with 953120 on `RESPONSE_BODY`, for the wound phrase, with or without a search term. The list half has its own row, `waf-clinical-text-corpus-tickler-list-response` (2026-10-08, same install): an owned tickler whose message holds the corpus (set by SQL, so the request side is not involved) is listed with a plain search term, and the GET `/tickler/ListTicklers` is dropped with 953120 on `RESPONSE_BODY` and no answer reaching the client; with the wound phrase and the `../` phrase left out, the same row passes and the message comes back exactly. Static: none, a fix may be a response-side exclusion or an encoder change. Filed as #4574. **Fixed (2026-10-14, #4574):** Phase-3 rules 1072/1073 exclude only 953120 RESPONSE_BODY for HTTP 200 application/json replies on POST Measurements and GET ListTicklers. Other routes, methods, MIME types, status codes and outbound detectors retain inspection. Both real browser rows reproduced the dropped response under the old policy and passed with exact saved/echoed text on 2026-10-14. Both expected-failure pins are removed. | fixed |
| 213 | **A GET whose search term holds `../` is refused by the WAF even where the argument is exempt, because the path-traversal rules also score the request URI.** Exclusion 1141 (`REQUEST-900-EXCLUSION-RULES-BEFORE-CRS.conf:1481`) unhooks six tag families from `ARGS:search[value]` on `GET /tickler/ListTicklers`. CRS 930100 and 930110 (`attack-lfi`) also test `REQUEST_URI_RAW`, `REQUEST_URI` and `REQUEST_FILENAME`, and on a GET the search term is in the query string, which is part of the URI; a per-argument `ctl` cannot reach those variables. The row keeps the fixture tickler plain, so nothing in the answer can trip a response rule; the saved message coming back is finding 212's `tickler-list-response` row. The tickler expected-failure pin is removed and encounter entry has its own regression row. | fixed |
| 214 | **A scope list sent the standard way, with `%20`, is stored as one unknown scope.** `OAuth1ParamParser.parseFromRequest` reads a query value through `decode` (`:89-90`, `:134`), which turns only `+` into a space ("let container handle full decode", and nothing later does), so `scope=demographic.read%20provider.read` reaches `OscarRequestTokenService` (`:146-149`) as the single token `demographic.read%20provider.read`. With `oauth.scope.enforcement.enabled` unset (the shipped default) it is stored on the request token and then on the access token, so every later scope check reads an unknown scope; with enforcement on, `validateRequestedScopes` answers 400 `invalid_scope` for a request that follows RFC 5849 section 3.6, which encodes a space as `%20`, and only a client that sends `+` can ask for two scopes | Live, `oauth-rest-surfaces-scope-list` (2026-10-08, packaged install through `:443`): a signed POST `/ws/oauth/initiate?scope=demographic.read%20provider.read` is answered 200 and `ServiceRequestToken.scopes` holds `demographic.read%20provider.read`; the full handshake with the same list ends with `ServiceAccessToken.scopes` = `demographic.read%20provider.read` (the first version of `oauth-rest-surfaces`, which asked for a list, failed its control on exactly that). `oauth-rest-surfaces` therefore requests the one scope `demographic.read`, so its handshake stays valid once finding 150 is fixed Fixed on release/2026.08 by #4461 (`OAuthScopes.parseScopeString` decodes the stored scope list once). Re-verified live on the 2026.09.0~snapshot26 package (release/2026.08 at 0c43d2f862): the step that was pinned (a request for two scopes is stored as two scopes) passes; the release's `oauth-rest-surfaces` now requests the list in scoped mode. | `fixed` |
| 215 | **The Penicillin and Sulfonamide allergy shortcuts file the allergy under DrugRef ids that name other products.** `ChooseAllergy2.jsp:121,125` and `ShowAllergies2.jsp:477,486` post the fixed ids `44452` (Penicillin) and `44159` (Sulfonamide) with type 10, the drug class. In the packaged DrugRef (`drugref2.cd_drug_search`) 44452 is a multivitamin brand (category 12) and 44159 is `FLUOXETINE ... 25MG` (category 11); the classes are `PENICILLINS` (41472) and `SULFONAMIDES` (41258, 41311), category 10. `RxAddAllergy2Action` looks the posted id up and saves what comes back, so the allergy's `drugref_id` points at another product and the identifiers of the class are never stored. This is the cause of finding 178's symptom on this install: the lookup finds a record, so nothing throws and nothing is logged | Live, `allergy-add-penicillin-shortcut-id` (2026-10-08, packaged DrugRef): the form's ID field and the saved row's `drugref_id` are 44452; `SELECT id,category,name FROM drugref2.cd_drug_search WHERE id IN (44452,44159)` returns the vitamin brand and the fluoxetine product. The Sulfonamide shortcut is read from source and the same query, not driven Re-checked live on the 2026.09.0~snapshot26 package (release/2026.08 at 0c43d2f862) after #4446 and #4455 merged: still reproduces (`allergy-add-penicillin-shortcut-id` fails at its pinned step). Filed as #4576. **Fixed (2026-10-15, #4576):** as the clinical lead asked on the issue, the class is named by its ATC code: penicillins J01C, sulfonamides J01E (`AllergyAtcClass.Shortcut`, which both pages render the shortcut's values from). The allergy is saved with TYPECODE 8 (ATC Class), the class code in `atc` and no `drugref_id`. `RxDrugData.getAllergyWarnings` decides such an allergy from its code: a drug whose ATC code starts with the class code warns, and a code that places it outside the class is checked and clear. DrugRef alone is not enough, because it resolves a class by name: this extract has no ATC class named PENICILLINS, and the one named SULFONAMIDES is G01AE (gynecological), so it would miss Septra. DrugRef is still asked about every shortcut allergy, under both of the class's names (the AHFS class the old shortcut used and the ATC class), and any warning it returns is kept. That keeps the products the DPD files outside the ATC class but under the AHFS one: on this extract HP-PAC (A02BD07), sulfasalazine (A07EC01), D10AB52, G01AE10, J01RA02 and sulfacetamide/prednisolone eye drops (S01CA02). A review of the first version of the fix found that they would otherwise have read as checked and clear. A drug coded above the class (J01 against J01C) leaves DrugRef's answer as it is. Allergies the old shortcuts recorded (`drugref_id` 44452 or 44159, TYPECODE 10, the shortcut's description; OSCAR posted the same ids) are not rewritten in place: the check reads them as their ATC class, and an amendment saves them in the new form. Live on a 2026.09.0~snapshot26 package built from the fix branch and installed into an Ubuntu 26.04 container (`carlos-ctl check` clean, `:443`): `allergy-add-penicillin-shortcut-id` passes with its expectedFailure removed, and the new `allergy-class-shortcut-alert` passes. In that check Amoxil (J01CA04) warns on the shortcut's penicillin allergy and on an old shortcut's, and on no sulfonamide one. Septra (J01EE01) warns on both sulfonamide allergies only. HP-PAC warns on both penicillin allergies and BLEPHAMIDE on both sulfonamide allergies. Biaxin (J01FA09) warns on none and leaves none "not checked". An amended old Sulfa allergy is stored as J01E. With the pre-fix `RxDrugData` classes swapped into the installed webapp, the check fails at the Amoxil step (only the old shortcut's row warns, through DrugRef's AHFS name). With the pre-fix pages and actions swapped in too, it fails at its first step. With the first version's `RxDrugData` (ATC prefix only for new allergies), it fails at the HP-PAC step. Follow-up #4680 (2026-10-15): phc007 explicitly retained the trimethoprim alert. With ingredient matching approved for combinations/topicals, the exact DIN supplies active ingredients when a shortcut does not match the ATC prefix. Tests without AHFS cover HP-PAC, sulfacetamide, sulfasalazine and silver sulfadiazine, with unrelated same-branch and non-antibiotic sulfonamide controls. Missing product/ingredient data is reported as unchecked. Existing ATC and old shortcut rows use the same rules; stored allergies are not rewritten. | fixed |
| 216 | **Opening the MCEDT Upload tab never stages a generated OHIP claim file into `ONEDT_OUTBOX`; only files added by hand are ever there.** `ActionUtils.getOutboxTimestamp` (`mailbox/ActionUtils.java:364-382`) reads the time of the last open from `<ONEDT_OUTBOX>/.timestamp` (`validateGeneratedChildPath`, `:371`), but `setOutboxTimestamp` (`:385-397`) writes it through `PathValidationUtils.validateGeneratedSiblingPath(ONEDT_OUTBOX, ".timestamp", ...)` (`:389`), which appends the suffix to the directory's NAME (`PathValidationUtils.java:568-590`), so the file written is `<parent>/outbox.timestamp`. The file that is read is therefore always empty: `readLine()` returns null, `.trim()` throws a NullPointerException (`:374`), the catch logs it at ERROR (`:380`) and `startDate` stays at "now". `Upload2Action.execute` (`:98-110`) then calls `moveOhipToOutBox` for the window between "now" and a few milliseconds later (`ActionUtils.java:290-316`), which matches no file, so a claim file the diskette wrote to HOME_DIR is never copied. (OBEC files are copied when they are generated, `ObecData.writeFile`, so only OHIP claim files are affected.) Each open leaves an empty `outbox/.timestamp` and writes `onEDTDocs/outbox.timestamp` (16 bytes, `dd-MM-yyyy HH:mm`), and the journal logs `ERROR mailbox.ActionUtils (ActionUtils.java:380) - Unable to open outbox timestamp file` with `NullPointerException: Cannot invoke "String.trim()" because the return value of "java.io.BufferedReader.readLine()" is null` at `ActionUtils.java:374`. | 218 | **MCEDT Delete Selected is not atomic, and it never reports a delete that failed.** `Upload2Action.deleteUpload` (`:340-357`) validates each name of the list inside the loop (`validatedOutboxFile`, `:346`) and deletes it at once (`file.delete()`, `:347`), so in a list `valid,../x` the valid file is already gone when the second name is refused and the page reports 'Invalid filename: must not include a path'. | 220 | **The MCEDT Change Password page shows the user name `null` when `mcedt.service.user` is not set.** `User2Action.execute` (`:63`) stores `CarlosProperties.getProperty("mcedt.service.user")` in the session, `CarlosProperties.getProperty` returns the key's default when it is absent (`:144-170`) and no default exists for it, so the value is `null` and `setAttribute` removes the attribute. `changepass.jsp` (`:45`, `:132`) reads it back and prints it with a bare scriptlet, `value="<%=mcedtUsername%>"`, which writes the four letters `null` into the read-only User Name field. Low severity (cosmetic, and the field is read-only), and the scriptlet is unencoded, which `CLAUDE.md` rules out for any value | Source: the lines cited above; `PROPERTY_DEFAULTS` holds no `mcedt.*` key. Live control (2026-10-09, packaged install through `:443`): the packaged `carlos.properties` ships `mcedt.service.user=` (set, empty), `GET /mcedt/kaichpass` answers a User Name field with `value=""`, so the `null` case was not driven; it needs the key removed from the properties file and a service restart, which this install does not allow. Filed as #4581. | issue-filed |
| 221 | **MCEDT Change Password checks nothing on the server: a blank password, no password at all, or a confirmation that does not match is stored.** `User2Action.changePassword` (`:72-88`) saves `getPassword()` as the `mcedt_account_password` property whatever it holds and then sets `isPassChange` to `true`; `conPassword` is not bound to any property of the action (the unused `confirmPassword` field is a different name), and the blank and mismatch checks exist only in `changepass.jsp` (`onSub`, `:74-93`). A request that skips the page, or a browser with script off, replaces the working credential for a provincial claims service with an empty or mistyped one and is told 'EDT Password has been changed successfully'. The project's rule is that server-side validation is authoritative and browser checks are only feedback (`CLAUDE.md`, login and password-reset work) | Live (2026-10-09, packaged install through `:443`, scratch probe with a valid token; the clinic's own rows were parked and restored, the install held none): `POST /mcedt/kaichpass` `method=changePassword` with `password=` empty and a different `conPassword` answered HTTP 200 and left one `mcedt_account_password` row whose value is empty; the same POST with no `password` parameter left a row with a NULL value; `password=A` with `conPassword=B` stored A. Source: `User2Action.java:72-88`, `changepass.jsp:74-93`. Filed as #4582. | issue-filed |
| 222 | **`CaseManagementEntry2Action.print()` returns `null` after streaming the chart PDF (and `ManageEForm2Action.exportEForm` after streaming the eForm ZIP), where the project rule is an explicit `NONE`.** `print()` writes the whole chart to `response.getOutputStream()` through `CaseManagementPrint.doPrint` (`:3089`) and, on a failure before any byte, calls `sendError` (`:3070`, `:3098`); it returns `null` after both (`:3071`, `:3102`). `CLAUDE.md` 'Direct Response Actions' requires `return NONE` after a direct response, because a bare `null` is not reliable enough to keep a named result from being resolved after the bytes are out; the rest of this class returns `NONE` after its direct responses (`:250`, `:259`, `:2230`, `:2238`, `:2871`). `ManageEForm2Action.exportEForm` has the same gap: it streams the eForm export ZIP through `response.getOutputStream()` (`EFormExportZip.exportForms`, `:87`) and returns `null` (`:88`). Low severity: Struts 7.1.1 accepts a `null` result code without an exception, so nothing visible breaks on this build | Source: the lines cited above. Live control: the Print icon ▸ Print All Notes ▸ Print downloaded a valid PDF (`%PDF-` header and `%%EOF` trailer) in `audit-log-chart-read` as it stood at `a5a8c10ac7` (2026-10-08, packaged install), so the defect is the missing guarantee, not lost output. The eForm export is the same: `export-content-eform-export-zip` downloaded and unzipped the exports of three owned forms intact (2026-10-09, packaged install). Not pinned by a check: a PDF or ZIP that stays intact cannot fail on it. Filed as #4583. | issue-filed |
| 223 | **Save rolls a future Encounter Date back to the current time and tells the clinician nothing: `encounter.futureDate.Msg` ("Observation Date set in future, rolled back to current time") only reaches the screen after Sign & Save or Verify & Sign has already saved and signed the note.** `CaseManagementEntry2Action.noteSave` replaces a posted `observation_date` later than now with the current time and stores the message in the request attribute `DateError` (`:1673`). `save()` (`:1850`) then returns the chain result `list`, which `struts-clinical.xml:175` maps to `/CaseManagementView?method=view`; nothing in that view reads `DateError`. Its only readers are `newEncounterLayout.jsp:506` (a full chart load), `noteIssueList.jsp:473` (after `ajaxsave`) and `closeWithError.jsp:34` (after `saveAndExit`, `:2170`). The browser only refuses a day after today (`newCaseManagementView.js.jsp:2609` `validDate()`, alert "Observation date must be in the past"), and the Encounter Date calendar fills 12:00 when a day is picked, so picking today before noon posts a time later today and Save silently stores the current time. Sign & Save with such a date saves and signs the note, then leaves the chart window on the bare `closeWithError.jsp` heading instead of closing it. Low severity: the stored time is the current time, never the future one, and nothing is lost | Live on the packaged install (alpha19, 2026-10-09, `echart-note-verify-appointment-status`): picking today in the calendar at 01:59 posted `observation_date=09-Oct-2026 12:00`, Save answered 200 with no alert, no message in the response body or the chart page, and the note was stored at 2026-10-09 01:59:09 (the database clock). With the posted date rewritten to tomorrow 09:00 the result was the same, and with Sign & Save the note was stored signed at the current time while the window showed only the message. Pinned by the final step of `echart-note-verify-appointment-status`. Filed as #4584. **Alpha20 fix:** #4905: future dates are refused before note mutation/persistence with localized AJAX/full-form feedback and retained drafts. Live echart-future-date-rejection passes Save, Sign & Save, Verify & Sign and a valid-date retry; date unit cases include note-switch save. | fixed |
| 224 | **Sign & Save and Verify & Sign archive the appointment after changing its status, so the `appointmentArchive` row holds the new status and the one it replaced is kept nowhere.** `CaseManagementManagerImpl.saveCaseManagementNote` sets the new status (`:2353` verify, `:2372` sign) and only then calls `appointmentArchiveDao.archiveAppointment(appointment)` (`:2384`), which copies the entity as it now is (`AppointmentArchiveDaoImpl:53-57`). The writers that do archive the version they replace do it before the change: `AppointmentUpdateRecord2Action:100` before its edits, `AppointmentStatusTransitionService:97-98` and `AppointmentManagerImpl:216-218` before `setStatus`, the BC `BillingSaveBilling2Action:579-581` before the billed status, and the legacy `EctSaveEncounter2Action:276-278` and `:286-288` before `setStatus(signStatus())` / `setStatus(verifyStatus())`. Other status writers, the Ontario bill save among them, do not archive at all (finding 228). After a chart sign the appointment's history therefore reads `tS` twice (the archive row and the current row) and `t` never, and the archive row keeps the booking's old `updatedatetime` beside the new status. Low to medium severity: the status history of every signed visit is wrong | Live on the packaged install (alpha19, 2026-10-09): an appointment booked as `t` and signed with Sign & Save from its chart, `appointment.status` = `tS` and the one `appointmentArchive` row for it has status `tS` (updatedatetime of the booking, 02:03:09, against the appointment's 02:03:13). Pinned by `echart-note-verify-appointment-status-archive` (the same script with `ECHART_VERIFY_PIN=archive`) Filed as #4585. **Fixed (#4585, PR #4694):** the service locks and refreshes the current appointment, validates patient ownership before clinical writes, saves the note revision, then archives the unchanged appointment before changing its status/audit fields. All writes share the service transaction; lookup/locking failures propagate. On 2026-10-15 the native archive workflow passes Sign & Save and Verify & Sign, checking exact old fields, one new archive per save, unchanged previous archives and the untouched second appointment. The 275-check Java selection includes real H2 commit/late-failure rollback and cached-row/competing-lock cases. No archive expected-failure waiver remains. | fixed |
| 225 | **The CPP item editor's Assign Issues box takes the clinician's pick and throws it away: the item is saved linked to its own CPP issue only.** `newEncounterLayout.jsp:790-793` renders the box (`#issueAutocompleteCPP`, class `issueAutocomplete`, label "Assign Issues") inside the CPP item form `frmIssueNotes`, and `ChartNotes.jsp:676-702` binds the jQuery UI autocomplete to every `.issueAutocomplete`; its `select` handler writes the pick into `#newIssueId` and `#newIssueName` (`ChartNotes.jsp:532-533`), which belong to the chart's note form `caseManagementEntryForm`, not to the CPP form. | 226 | **The Unresolved Issues and Resolved Issues module headings do nothing: their click handler is a syntax error.** `EctDisplayIssues2Action:61` and `EctDisplayResolvedIssues2Action:58` give the heading the statement `$('check_issue').value='';document.caseManagementViewForm.submit();` through `setLeftURL`, and `LeftNavBarDisplay.jsp:187` writes `dao.getLeftURL() + "; return false;"` into the heading's `onclick` through the `javaScriptAttribute` encoder. That encoder is for a value inside a JavaScript string literal, so every `'` becomes the text `\x27`, which outside a string literal is not JavaScript: the attribute reads `$(\x27check_issue\x27).value=\x27\x27;...` and the browser reports `Invalid or unexpected token`. These are the only two headings that use `setLeftURL` (the others use `setLeftPopup`, which the page writes inside quoted literals). | 228 | **At least three appointment status writers change the row without writing any `appointmentArchive` row, the Ontario bill save among them, so the status they replace is kept nowhere.** (1) The Ontario billing save: `BillingOnSave2Action:161` calls `BillingClaimSubmissionService.updateApptStatus` (`:306-307`), which calls `BillingOnLookupService.updateApptStatus` (`:468-479`): `appt.setStatus(status)`, `setLastUpdateUser`, `setUpdateDateTime`, `appointmentDao.merge(appt)`, no archive. Every appointment billed from the day sheet's B link goes through it, so the status it had before billing (To Do, Here, Picked, ...) is in no history row, and the appointment's updatedatetime moves on with nothing recording what changed. (2) `CaseManagementEntry2Action.issueNoteSaveJson`, when the request carries `sign=true` and `signAndExit=true` (`:946-953`), runs `appointment.setStatus(statusData.signStatus())` and `appointmentDao.merge(appointment)` with no archive; no page or script in the webapp posts `method=issueNoteSaveJson` or `signAndExit`, so it is reachable only by a direct POST of an authenticated provider who holds the note lock. (3) `PhsStarHandler.updateAppointmentStatus` (`:514`, status set at `:546`, merge at `:548`) changes the status of an appointment from a STAR HL7 message, and `PhsStarHandler.rescheduleAppointment` (`:427`, merge at `:495`) moves its provider, date, time, location and type, both with no archive (the file never mentions the archive DAO). These are the writers found, not a complete list. The writers that archive do it before the change (finding 224 lists them), which is what makes `appointmentArchive` the history of replaced versions; these leave a gap in it. Low to medium severity: the first is an everyday path, the second has no UI entry and the third only runs on a deployment fed by a STAR HL7 interface | Live for (1), on the packaged install (alpha19, 2026-10-09, `echart-note-verify-appointment-status-billing`): an owned FAKE To Do (`t`) appointment billed through the day sheet B link, the OHIP form (A007A, diagnostic code 250, an owned billing physician), Next and Save: one claim saved, `appointment.status` = `B`, and `appointmentArchive` held no row for the appointment (the check read an empty list). Source for (2) and (3): the lines cited above; `grep -n archive PhsStarHandler.java` finds nothing, and `grep -rn "issueNoteSaveJson\|signAndExit" src/main` finds only `CaseManagementEntry2Action`; those two are not driven. (1) is pinned by `echart-note-verify-appointment-status-billing` (the same script with `ECHART_VERIFY_PIN=billing`) Filed as #4589. Fixed for #4589: `BillingOnLookupService.updateApptStatus`, `CaseManagementEntry2Action.issueNoteSaveJson` and `PhsStarHandler` (`updateAppointmentStatus`, `rescheduleAppointment`) archive the appointment before changing it. | fixed |
| 229 | **A role with chart read and no note or issue right can change a chart issue's state; the "refusal" comes after the write.** `CaseManagementEntry2Action.execute` checks only `_demographic` r (`:154-156`) before dispatching `issueList` (`:186-187`), `issueAdd` (`:192-193`), `issueDelete` (`:200-201`) and `issueChange` (`:202-203`). `issueChange` (`:2737-2800`) checks only that the session has a `userrole` (`:2744-2748`), sets acute, certain, major and resolved from the request's `issueCheckList[N].issue.*` parameters (`:2773-2785`) and saves them through `caseManagementMgr.saveAndUpdateCaseIssues` (`:2790`), which checks nothing. It needs the form bean the chart's own load stores in the session, and `edit()` (`:275`), which builds it, has the same `userrole`-only check (`:284`). The class has no `_casemgmt.notes` or `_casemgmt.issues` write check anywhere (its one `_casemgmt.notes` check, `:2829`, is a read check in `notehistory`); the `_casemgmt.issues` w right exists and gates only `IssueSearch.jsp:42-44` and the Add New Issue button of `CaseManagementEntry.jsp:392-394`. `writeAccess` only disables controls (`noteIssueList.jsp:285-286`, `:371-372`), and not the Acute and Resolved radios. The only gate on this path is the view the result renders: `noteIssueList.jsp:51-54` redirects to `securityError?type=_casemgmt.notes` for a role without `_casemgmt.notes` r, after `issueChange` has committed (the same shape as finding 198), and only when the request carries `ajax=true`, as the chart's own does (`:2815-2816`). Without it the result is `view`, which renders `CaseManagementEntry.jsp`; that page has no `_casemgmt.notes` r redirect (a role without `_casemgmt.notes` u gets the line "You do not have permission to edit this note." in a 200 page, `:481-483`), so a direct POST of `issueChange` without `ajax=true` is not refused even late (not driven). `issueAdd` (`:2421`, `userrole` check `:2426`) and `issueDelete` (`:2672`, check `:2676-2679`) have the same one-line check, and the same late gate only for `ajax=true` (`:2547`, `:2731`; otherwise `view`, `:2548`, `:2732`, with no refusal at all); they were not driven live. Medium severity: a role that may not read or write the chart's notes can resolve a patient's problem, which moves it out of the Issues module, or flip its acute, certain and major flags, and the response says it was refused | Live, `authz-write-issue-change` (the `authz-write-role-matrix` script with `AUTHZ_WRITE_MODE=issue-change`, which pins this finding), step "chart issue change: the restricted login's issueChange with its own valid token is refused and the issue row is unchanged" (the fixture, the control and the POST are the steps before it): the write-restricted login (`_demographic` r, `_appointment` w, `_eChart` r, in the test login's programs) opened an owned patient's chart, which stores the session's form bean, and sent the `issueChange` the full login's chart had sent (captured from its note editor, with the checklist revealed by a script because finding 227 hides it, plus `issueCheckList[0].issue.major=true`) with its own valid CSRF token. The answer was HTTP 302 to `/carlos/securityError`, which `h.assertRefused` accepts as a refusal, and the owned `casemgmt_issue` row went from acute 0, certain 0, major 0, resolved 0 and its seeded `update_date` to major 1 and resolved 1, so the row-unchanged half failed (COUNT 1 to 0). The same request from the full login wrote the same two flags (control). The journal logs nothing for the `issueChange` request itself; the errors in that window are the known ones from opening the restricted chart (findings 142 and 201) Authorization defect for a signed-in user: tracked in umbrella [#4223](https://github.com/carlos-emr/carlos/issues/4223), section A. **Alpha20 fix:** #4905: issue and diagnosis mutations require issue-write and patient access before session or persistent changes. Live authz-write-issue-change passes the full-access control and restricted valid-token replay with every issue flag unchanged; the expectedFailure pin is removed. | fixed |

### Found by the Ontario form catalogue

From `form-catalog-smoke` and the Mental Health, Form 14, Form 42 and Annual V2 rows added to
`clinical-forms-save-reopen` (Task 30 of the 2026.08 coverage plan), run on the packaged Ontario
install (`carlos-emr 2026.08.0~alpha19`, demo data) at 2e102bb8f8. Each form was opened from a
marker-named Forms-menu registration of its shipped `form_value`, typed into, saved, reopened from a
fresh chart and printed, on an owned FAKE patient with a complete demographic record (see finding 241
for why the record has to be complete). "Live" marks what the run showed; the check that pins each
finding is named in its evidence cell.

| # | Defect | Evidence | Status |
|---|---|---|---|
| 230 | **The ALPHA form (Forms menu "ALPHA") cannot be opened: it answers HTTP 500 for every patient.** `formalpha.jsp:65-72` asks `FrmRecordFactory` for the record class `Alpha` and calls `getFormRecord` on the result. `Alpha` is not in `FrmRecordFactory.ALLOWED_FORM_CLASSES` and no `FrmAlphaRecord` class exists, so `factory()` returns `null` and the page throws a `NullPointerException` (`Cannot invoke "FrmRecord.getFormRecord(...)" because "rec" is null`). The `formAlpha` table and the shipped registration exist, so a clinician who enables the entry reaches an error page. `Frm2Action` carries a comment naming this exact case ("the form selector offers ALPHA and formAlpha carries rows, but no FrmAlphaRecord exists") and answers a Save with 400, but the page that would post is never rendered | Live: ALPHA opened from the Forms menu, HTTP 500 and the CARLOS error page; the journal shows `NullPointerException ... "rec" is null` for `uri=/carlos/form/formalpha`. Source: the lines cited above; `ls src/main/java/io/github/carlos_emr/carlos/form/FrmAlphaRecord.java` finds nothing. Pinned by `form-catalog-smoke-alpha`. Filed as #4590. **Fixed (2026-10-22, #4590):** `FrmAlphaRecord` reads and saves `formAlpha` and the record factory allows it; `formalpha.jsp` encodes every stored value and no longer links the missing stylesheet. `form-catalog-smoke-alpha` passes on debs built from the fix (Ubuntu 26.04); the expected-failure pin is removed. | fixed |
| 231 | **Thirteen legacy questionnaires forget every ticked box: saved checkbox answers are not shown again on the page Save redisplays or when the saved form is reopened, and saving again from that page overwrites them with blanks.** Nineteen record classes (`Frm2MinWalkRecord`, `FrmCESDRecord`, `FrmCaregiverRecord`, `FrmCostQuestionnaireRecord`, `FrmFallsRecord`, `FrmGripStrengthRecord`, `FrmHomeFallsRecord`, `FrmIntakeInfoRecord`, `FrmInternetAccessRecord`, `FrmLateLifeFDIDisabilityRecord`, `FrmLateLifeFDIFunctionRecord`, `FrmRhImmuneGlobulinRecord`, `FrmSF36CaregiverRecord`, `FrmSF36Record`, `FrmSatisfactionScaleRecord`, `FrmSelfAdministeredRecord`, `FrmSelfEfficacyRecord`, `FrmSelfManagementRecord`, `FrmTreatmentPrefRecord`) turn a `TINYINT` column into the attribute text `checked='checked'` only when `md.getColumnTypeName(i).equalsIgnoreCase("TINY")` (for example `FrmFallsRecord:97`, `FrmCESDRecord:87`). The shipped MySQL driver (`mysql-connector-j-9.6.0`) does not name the type `TINY`, so the branch never runs and the `else` branch writes the raw column value `1` into the tag: the page contains `<input type="checkbox" name="fallenLast12MY" class="checkbox" 1="">`. `FrmRecordHelp:73`, which the working forms use, compares with `TINYINT`. Every Save stores the ticked boxes correctly, so the row is right and only the display is wrong; the harm is the next Save from the blank-looking page, which files a version with every box unticked, and that version is the one the chart opens (`formId=latest`) | Live: the check types a value and ticks box(es) on each form, Save stores them (the row holds `1`), and the redisplayed form and the reopened form show them unticked, on CESD, Caregiver, Caregiver - SF36, SF36, Cost Questionnaire, Falls History, HOME FAST, Intake Information, FDI Disability, FDI Function, Risk Assessment, Self Management (its checkboxes are on page 4) and Treatment Preference; the text and number answers on the same forms are shown correctly. Probe on Falls History: first Save stores `fallenLast12MY`=1, the redisplayed box is unticked, a second Save from that window files the next version with `fallenLast12MY`=0. Of the nineteen classes, `formGripStrength` and `formSelfEfficacy` have no `TINYINT` columns (their answers are text), so the comparison changes nothing there; four more have `TINYINT` columns and are not in the catalogue, so were not driven (2 Minute Walk, Internet Access, Rh Immune Globulin, Satisfaction Scale). Pinned by `form-catalog-smoke-checkbox-restore`. Tracked in #4466. **Fixed (2026-10-19, #4466):** the nineteen record classes now ask `FrmRecordHelp.isBooleanColumnType`, the check the working forms already used. It accepts the `BIT` name Connector/J gives `tinyint(1)` and the `TINYINT` name it gives other widths, so a saved tick is shown again and the next Save keeps it. The expected-failure pin is removed. The four classes the catalogue does not reach (2 Minute Walk, Internet Access, Rh Immune Globulin, Satisfaction Scale) are checked by `form-checkbox-reopen`, which opens a seeded saved record of each and asserts the ticked, unticked and untouched boxes. | fixed |
| 232 | **Saving the CESD questionnaire ends on the CARLOS error page (HTTP 404) even though the row is saved.** `formCESD.jsp:58` posts `form_link` = `formCDSD.jsp` (the letters transposed). After the save `Frm2Action` redirects to `form/forwardname?form_link=formCDSD.jsp&...`, `FormViewRoutes.resolveInternalViewFromFormLink` accepts the name and `FormForwardNamed2Action` forwards to `/WEB-INF/jsp/form/formCDSD.jsp`, which does not exist. A clinician sees an error after every Save of this form and has no sign that the answers were stored | Live: CESD Save stored one `formCESD` row with the typed answers, then the window showed the error page with HTTP 404 on `/carlos/form/forwardname`; the journal shows `errorpage.jsp triggered ... uri=/carlos/form/forwardname, status=404`. Source: `ls src/main/webapp/WEB-INF/jsp/form/formCDSD.jsp` finds nothing. Pinned by `form-catalog-smoke-cesd-redisplay`. Filed as #4591. **Fixed (2026-10-22, #4591):** `form_link` names `formCESD.jsp`, so Save returns to the saved form. `form-catalog-smoke-cesd-redisplay` passes on debs built from the fix; the expected-failure pin is removed. | fixed |
| 233 | **The Letterhead form adds a newline and four spaces to its Comments every time it is redisplayed, so each Save from a redisplayed or reopened letter lengthens it.** `formConsultant.jsp:268-270` puts the encoded comments on their own line inside the `<textarea>` and closes the tag on the next, indented, line, so the textarea's value is the comments plus `\n` and four spaces (the browser drops only the first newline). Save posts that value back. Low severity: the letter grows by whitespace, but it is a patient letter and a signature block can end up pages down | Live: the typed Comments are 55 characters; the redisplayed and the reopened textarea hold 60 (the same text and `\n` plus four spaces). Source: the lines cited above. Pinned by `form-catalog-smoke-letterhead-restore`. Filed as #4609. **Fixed (#4609):** Enabled session-scoped JDBC batching, cached the four compiled templates and static backgrounds, and added Save/Print feedback. The progress popup is replaced by an inline PDF viewer. Focused rendering also verifies cached templates do not reuse patient data. Timings are diagnostic, not a budget. | fixed |

| 256 | **A lab result whose first five characters are all digits or `+ , - . / : ; < = > ?` is filed in `measurements` cut to its first four characters, so the flowsheets, the Measurements module and the Health Tracker show a different number from the lab.** The packaged `carlos.properties:604` sets `HL7_LAB_MEASUREMENT_FILTER` (its comment calls it the default that "will filter out 90% of all Excelleris lab results"), and one of its alternatives is `^([+-\\?]{1,4})`, which stands before the numeric alternative. In a character class `+-\?` is the range from `+` to `?` (0x2B to 0x3F: the digits, `.`, `,`, `-`, `/`, `:`, `;`, `<`, `=`, `>` and `?`), not the three characters it appears to list, and `{1,4}` takes up to four of them. `Hl7textResultsData.populateMeasurementsTable` compiles the property (`:138-139`), runs `matcher.find()` on the result (`:232`) and stores `matcher.group()` as the measurement value (`:236`), so a result whose first five characters are all digits or `+ , - . / : ; < = > ?` is stored as its first four: `0.095` becomes `0.09`, `10.55` becomes `10.5`, `112.5` becomes `112.`, `12345` becomes `1234`, `<0.05` becomes `<0.0`, `>100.5` becomes `>100` and `1,250` becomes `1,25`. A result of four characters or fewer, or one with a character outside that set within its first five (`100 mmol` is filed as `100`), is not cut, and one that begins with a valid date is filed whole (the date alternatives come first). The cause is the SHIPPED default, not a clinic setting: `src/main/resources/carlos.properties:604` is copied into the WAR, `debian/rules:331-332` copies it into the package skeleton, and `carlos-emr.postinst:551` installs it once as `/etc/carlos-emr/carlos.properties`, where the installed copy holds the same line. An HbA1c a lab reports as a fraction (`0.078`) is stored as `0.07`; the lab report itself still shows the full value | Live: a probe filed one CML lab through Inbox, Create Lab (2026-10-09) with results 112.5 (3180), 10.55 (3120), 12345 (3140), 139 (3210) and 4.15 (3220); the stored HL7 text holds all five values and `measurements.dataField` holds `112.`, `10.5`, `1234`, `139` and `4.15`. `lab-to-flowsheet-long-value` files a creatinine of 112.5 (CML 3180, the flowsheet type SCR, so no A1C fix can decide the pin) and reads `112.`. The shipped property loaded with `java.util.Properties` and run through `java.util.regex` on the live install (2026-10-09) gives `0.095` to `0.09`, `12345` to `1234`, `<0.05` to `<0.0`, `>100.5` to `>100`, `1,250` to `1,25`, `123.4 mmol` to `123.`, and leaves `100 mmol` as `100`, `1250` whole and `2026-10-09` whole. Source: `src/main/resources/carlos.properties:604`; `Hl7textResultsData.java:138-139,232-236`. Pinned by `lab-to-flowsheet-long-value`. Filed as #4613. **Fixed (2026-10-15, #4613):** the shipped filter's `^([+-\\?]{1,4})` is replaced by `^[-+<>=]{0,2}\\s?[-+,./0-9:;<=>?]+`, which takes a result's whole leading run of those characters, and allows one space after a leading comparator or sign (`< 5` had been filed as `<`). The numeric alternative that ended the line, which the new one covers, is gone. Every trimmed result the old filter filed is still filed, and its value can only grow to what the lab sent: 3,000,000 random results through both expressions agreed on what is filed, and each new value starts with the old one. The result is now trimmed again after its `<br />` markers are removed, so `<br /> -5.2` is filed as `-5.2` (it had reached the filter with a leading space). `Hl7textResultsData` now leaves a match longer than `measurements.dataField` (255) out of `measurements` with a warning, where the packaged install (MariaDB `sql_mode=""`, `jdbcCompliantTruncation=false` in the JDBC URL) would have cut it. The carlos-emr postinst rewrites the exact old stock line in `/etc/carlos-emr/carlos.properties` to the skeleton's once, under the provisioning lock (sentinel `.lab-measurement-filter-migrated`; a key defined twice or a clinic's own value is left alone, and a definition in effect that still carries the old alternative without being the exact stock line, such as one with CRLF endings, is named in a warning), and `deb-upgrade-baseline.sh`/`deb-upgrade-verify.sh` sanction that one rewrite. Pinned by `Hl7textResultsDataUnitTest` (the shipped filter read from `carlos.properties`), `debian/assets/tests/test_lab_filter_migration.py` (the real postinst block run under `sh`) and `scripts/deb-upgrade-snapshot.test.js`. `lab-to-flowsheet-long-value` now expects every step to pass. It now files three more such results on the creatinine's lab (cholesterol 10.55, ALP 1,250, TSH <0.05), and its expected-failure pin is removed. Its target reader also unfolds the double-encoded tooltip that #4418 introduced, which had stopped every `lab-to-flowsheet` entry at step 1. On 2026-10-15, on Ubuntu 26.04: the 2026.08.0-alpha19 packages, installed, still filed the creatinine as `112.` (every earlier step passed). An upgrade to a `2026.09.0~snapshot26` package built from the fix rewrote the `/etc` line, printing its notice, and kept the file's mode and owner (`640 root:carlos`). `deb-upgrade-verify.sh` then passed (46 checks, including the sanctioned lab-filter rewrite and its sentinel), and `lab-to-flowsheet-long-value` passed. **Not repaired:** measurements filed before the fix keep their cut values (an A1C of 0.095 is still `0.09` in the flowsheet history); the fix applies to labs filed from the upgrade on, and the lab reports themselves were always right. | fixed |

| 258 | **A lab-sourced value on the Diabetes Flowsheet, the A1C history page and the Health Tracker cannot be traced back to its lab.** `Hl7textResultsData.populateMeasurementsTable` stores the lab number as `measurementsExt.lab_no` (`:264-268`) and the provider as `0` (`:235`), but no page that lists measurements reads it: the flowsheet row (`TemplateFlowSheetPage.jspf:842-845`) opens only `ViewAddMeasurementData?measurement=A1C&id=<measurement id>` (the Add Measurement form, with a Delete button) and a tooltip ending `Entered By:Automatic`; `DisplayHistory.jsp` lists Type, Provider, Measuring Instruction, Data, Comments, Observation Date, Date Entered and Delete; and the tracker's `.history-value` (`HealthTrackerPage.jspf:841`) carries a popover `Entered by: Automatic`.
